**Last Updated:** October 2, 2026 — the Helpdesk app started ([#303](https://github.com/Terrence721/platform-main/issues/303)): its scaffold landed and was renamed from `standalone-app` ([#857](https://github.com/Terrence721/platform-main/issues/857)); on October 1 the migrations review ([#106](https://github.com/Terrence721/platform-main/issues/106), 31/31 files) and type-level coverage ([#162](https://github.com/Terrence721/platform-main/issues/162), 13/13 modules) finished; see [Recent changes](#recent-changes) for what changed and when.

A phase-by-phase log of what's been done on this repo and what's still open. This is the source of truth for progress — the [GitHub Project board](https://github.com/users/Terrence721/projects/2) is a lighter-weight view of the same work, kept in sync with this file, not a separate source of truth.

**What this repo is:** a personal demonstration workspace, not a fork or continuation of the real [@ngrx/platform](https://github.com/ngrx/platform) project. It started as a copy of that project's root-level config, then built out module by module from there — adding the real, MIT-licensed source where fidelity to a battle-tested implementation matters, and deliberately redesigning specific pieces where the goal is a different, defensible architectural call. See [README.md](README.md) for the non-affiliation note and [LICENSE](LICENSE) for why the original copyright notice stays intact.

| `@ngrx/effects` module code review — 3rd of 13 modules                    | 18/18 source files reviewed; **0 real bugs found** — one correctness-critical ordering (`EffectsRootModule`'s subscribe-before-push, since `EffectSources`' plain `Subject` drops anything pushed before a subscriber exists) confirmed correct rather than assumed; one hypothesized bug (a thrown `store.dispatch()` silently killing the effects subscription) disproven by a throwaway repro against the real installed `rxjs`, not just reasoning — same "test it, don't just reason about it" discipline `store`'s review established                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          | Phase 45        |
| `@ngrx/router-store` module code review — 4th of 13 modules               | 12/12 source files reviewed; **7 real bugs found and fixed**, the highest hit rate of any module so far — two type-parameter-binding mistakes (`actions.ts`'s `storeState`/`routerState` swap, `router_store_config.ts`'s unparameterized `serializer` field), a missing barrel re-export (`index.ts`), two rounds of `RouterStateSelectors` fields missing `\| undefined` in `models.ts` (the second surfaced by `router_selectors.ts`'s review, in an already-closed file), a false JSDoc claim about reducer-thrown errors canceling navigation (`router_store_module.ts`, empirically disproven by un-skipping the one test that would have caught it), and `full_serializer.ts`'s `component` field silently sourced from the wrong object for `loadComponent`-based lazy routes. `store_router_connecting.service.ts` — the module's most central, complex file — got the deepest scrutiny and came back clean | Phase 46        |
| Restored real per-package `ng add @ngrx/X` resolution                     | Closed the fidelity tradeoff phase 38 named explicitly — all 11 modules now resolve `ng add @ngrx/store` etc. for real, delegating to the shared `modules/schematics` package rather than duplicating it. Surfaced and fixed 2 real Nx-remote-cache CI races along the way (redundant `push`+`pull_request` Quality runs; `quality.yml`/`pages.yml` racing each other on `main`). 14 PRs, all merged, `main` confirmed clean across multiple back-to-back merges afterward                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           | Phase 44–45     |
| `@ngrx/store-devtools` module code review — 5th of 13 modules             | 11/11 source files reviewed; **6 real bugs found and fixed plus 1 minor cleanup** — `config.ts`'s `createConfig()` mutated the caller's `features` object (two bugs that cancelled each other out), `extension.ts`'s fast path passed `nextActionId` instead of the processed action's id to a configured `actionSanitizer`, two barrel/type gaps (`index.ts`, the nullable `REDUX_DEVTOOLS_EXTENSION` token), `provide-store-devtools.ts`'s `IS_EXTENSION_OR_MONITOR_PRESENT` provided but never consumed (now wired up), and `reducer.ts`'s `TOGGLE_ACTION`/`SET_ACTIONS_ACTIVE` crashing on an id no longer staged — reachable from the public `toggleAction()` and able to take down an app's whole reactive state stream, not just the devtools panel                                                                                                                                                           | —               |

### Module additions

Real source added from a full local checkout of ngrx/platform, adapted per-module (version → `0.0.0`, `repository`/`bugs`/`homepage` → this repo, `pnpm exec` → bare commands for yarn). Package names keep the `@ngrx/*` scope on individual modules — the added source imports itself internally that way, and renaming would mean rewriting every internal cross-module import for no functional benefit (root package is already `private: true`).

### `Store` composition-over-inheritance redesign

**Why:** the real ngrx/platform `Store`/`ActionsSubject`/`ReducerManager` extend RxJS `Observable`/`BehaviorSubject`/`Subject` directly. That's a real Interface Segregation violation, not a style nitpick — it hands every consumer the entire RxJS operator surface (`pipe`, `lift`, `toPromise`, `forEach`, ...) when the actual contract is much narrower ("dispatch actions," "track the combined reducer"). `Store` even has to override `lift()` to keep RxJS's internal operator-chaining machinery from silently downgrading a `Store` back to a plain `Observable`. Composition — each class holds a private RxJS subject internally instead of extending one — closes that gap. Done one class at a time, in dependency order, each fully verified (tests/lint/build-package green) before moving to the next, since every module added afterward that actually depends on `@ngrx/store` — `entity`, `effects`, `operators`, `router-store`, `store-devtools`, `data` so far — depends on these three classes' public shape (`component-store` is the first exception: no `@ngrx/store` peer dependency at all).

| Phase | Commit    | Date       | What                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| ----- | --------- | ---------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| 16    | `7ae7781` | 2026-08-06 | `ActionsSubject`: composition over inheritance. Public surface deliberately hand-picked to match what an action bus actually needs (`next`, `subscribe`, `error`, `complete`, `asObservable`, `ngOnDestroy`) — no `pipe`/`lift`, not an `instanceof Observable`. Blast radius: one production call site (`state.ts`) + one test call site (`mock_store.spec.ts`) touched `ActionsSubject` as a raw Observable; both fixed. `store.spec.ts`'s direct `dispatcher.*` assertions needed no changes                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| 17    | `7ae7781` | 2026-08-06 | `ReducerManager`: same redesign. Cleaner seam than `ActionsSubject` — a separate `ReducerObservable` DI token already existed for consumers that need the Observable view, so only the provider wiring (`useExisting` → a `useFactory` calling `.asObservable()`) and `ReducerManager`'s own internals changed. One blast-radius miss on the first pass: `integration.spec.ts` injected `ReducerManager` directly instead of the `ReducerObservable` token and called `.pipe()` on it — caught by the test run, fixed by switching the injection to match the codebase's own established pattern. `MockReducerManager` needed no changes (extends `BehaviorSubject` directly, not `ReducerManager`)                                                                                                                                                                   |
| 18    | `7ae7781` | 2026-08-06 | `Store`: same redesign, last of the three. Unlike the other two, this one deliberately **removes** capability rather than just changing its implementation — `Store` no longer implements RxJS's `Observer<Action>` interface, so `next()`/`error()`/`complete()`/`subscribe()`/`pipe()` are gone; `select()` keeps its full overload set (now returning a plain `Observable` derived from the new `state$` field), and `state$` itself replaces "the raw store as an Observable." ~40 call sites across 6 spec files fixed; 2 tests deleted outright (tested removed capability, not adaptable), 2 more trimmed to their still-valid dispatcher-only half. The build itself (not grep) caught one real ripple: `MockStore`'s constructor already had its own private `state$: MockState<T>` field, colliding with `Store`'s new public one — renamed to `mockState$` |

### Security: CodeQL findings in the added schematics-core source

| Phase | Commit    | Date       | What                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| ----- | --------- | ---------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| 22    | `b77bcc6` | 2026-08-06 | [PR #21](https://github.com/Terrence721/platform-main/pull/21) fixed all 6 open CodeQL alerts in `ast-utils.ts`/`package.ts` (both the top-level `modules/schematics-core` copy and the duplicated nested `modules/store/schematics-core` copy — CodeQL only reported the nested one, but the vulnerable code exists in both). 5x `js/polynomial-redos`: two unanchored regexes containing `.*` (`symbolName.replace(/\..*$/, '')`, `symbolName.match(/\[(.*)\]/)`) — no `^` anchor means a non-matching input forces an O(n) retry from every character position, O(n²) total. Replaced with plain string methods (`split`/`indexOf`/`lastIndexOf`), immune by construction rather than just a safer regex. 1x `js/prototype-polluting-assignment`: `json[type][pkg] = version` with unvalidated keys — a `"__proto__"` value would reassign the object's prototype. Fixed with an explicit blocklist check. Real, pre-existing vulnerabilities in the added Angular CLI source (MIT-licensed, "Copyright Google Inc." in `ast-utils.ts`), not introduced by this repo — CodeQL found them here first. Landed via a real branch + PR rather than direct-to-`main`, matching `coolify-full`'s pattern of code-review-style fixes going through review, not scaffolding-style direct commits |

### CI reliability, CodeQL guard rewrite, and schematics-core consolidation

| Phase | Commit    | Date       | What                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| ----- | --------- | ---------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| 30    | `623ed2e` | 2026-08-07 | The `Set.has()` guard from PR #21 is functionally correct but CodeQL's `js/prototype-polluting-assignment` query doesn't trace booleans returned from `Set.has()`/`Array.includes()` back to the guarded key - re-scanning against the latest commit still flagged all 4 `schematics-core` copies as open. Rewrote to direct string-literal equality checks (the pattern CodeQL's own query recognizes) plus `Object.create(null)` for the nested object as belt-and-suspenders. All 6 open alerts closed as `fixed`.                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| 31    | `623ed2e` | 2026-08-07 | CI was intermittently failing ("Path .../migration.json does not exist", "Cannot find module '@ngrx/store'") - passed once, failed on 2 of 3 real runs. Two real, undeclared Nx task dependencies: each module's `build` target shelled out to `nx build-package X` as a raw command instead of a declared `dependsOn`, so Nx didn't guarantee its output was flushed before the next step; and `entity`/`effects`' `build-package` had no cross-project dependency on `store`, so it could run before `store`'s `ncp` step populated `node_modules/@ngrx/store`. Fixed with real `dependsOn` edges. Verified by stress-testing with a fully clean `dist`/`node_modules`/Nx cache and `--parallel=4` - no more races.            |
| 32    | `623ed2e` | 2026-08-07 | Consolidated the 4 duplicated `schematics-core` copies into `modules/schematics-core` - the reason for the duplication (independent npm publishing) doesn't apply to a repo that never publishes. Two real obstacles, not workarounds: TypeScript's `rootDir` rejected a file outside it needing emission (fixed by widening `rootDir`/`outDir` together for all 4 modules so `schematics-core` compiles once into `dist/modules/schematics-core`); an alias import type-checked fine but failed at runtime because ng-packagr's generated `package.json` `exports` map gates bare-specifier subpaths even when the file exists (fixed by keeping the import relative, which bypasses `exports` entirely). 12,453 lines removed. |

Uninstalled all 6, added to `.vscode/extensions.json`'s `unwantedRecommendations`.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |

Wired up Vitest's own first-party `html` reporter instead (`@vitest/ui` as a pinned devDependency), configured on the root aggregator config so `yarn test:report` covers every module in one pass.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| —     | `055659f` | 2026-08-07 | The report link 404'd on GitHub - `test-results/` is gitignored, and GitHub's blob viewer wouldn't render a committed HTML file live anyway without Pages behind it. Enabled GitHub Pages (Actions build source, via the API) and added `.github/workflows/pages.yml`: builds packages, runs the suite through the html reporter, deploys to Pages on every push to `main`. README now links to the live `https://terrence721.github.io/platform-main/` report.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| —     | `055659f` | 2026-08-07 | The report flagged 81-108 "slow" tests. First attempt (raising `slowTestThreshold`) was itself buggy twice: set it on `baseConfig` (only merges into each project's own config, not the root aggregator that owns the html reporter - no effect), then verified the fix by decoding the deployed report's actual `flatted`-serialized data instead of trusting the number on screen, twice. Once the config was confirmed genuinely applied, the count barely moved - which turned out to be the right signal to keep digging rather than stop. Found the real bug: `createWorkspace()` (`modules/schematics-core/testing/create-workspace.ts`) runs four full `@schematics/angular` generators, and all 9 spec files using it called it fresh in `beforeEach` instead of once - paying full generator cost before every single `it()`. Fixed by hoisting to `beforeAll` + Angular's built-in cheap in-memory `Tree.branch()` for per-test isolation. Measured: schematics tests 52.8s → 2.9s (18x), migrations 51.5s → 11.4s (4.5x); tests over 2000ms in those buckets went from 12 to 0. Also enabled `skipLibCheck` (safe, real, modest win - was never set, so `tsc`/`vue-tsc` fully re-checked every Angular/RxJS/NgRx `.d.ts` on every run; confirmed no hand-authored `.d.ts` files exist that this could hide bugs in). |
| —     | —         | 2026-08-07 | Investigated the remaining 8 slow tests (all `spec/types/**` compile-time type assertions) before accepting them as the floor. Confirmed via Vitest's own source (`node_modules/vitest/dist/chunks/index.UpGiHP7g.js`) that typecheck is already batched once per module (not per file) - no further batching win available. Vitest passes `--incremental --tsBuildInfoFile <path>` itself, but that path is `node_modules/vitest/dist/tsconfig.tmp.tsbuildinfo` - shared across every project unless running under Yarn PnP (this repo uses classic Yarn). Proved the collision with a real experiment: `effects` typecheck went 3.41s cold → 2.44s warm (same project, re-run) → 3.53s again after `store`'s run overwrote the shared cache file. A multi-project CI run makes this cache nearly useless by design, not by misconfiguration, and it isn't something exposed for us to fix from the config side. Concluded the 8 remaining tests are genuine per-assertion `tsc` cost for core-API type coverage (`createEffect`, `ofType`, `createAction`, `select`, `createActionGroup`, ...) - reducing them further would mean cutting real type-safety test coverage, not fixing a defect.                                                                                                                                 |

| — | `47ff1f5` | 2026-08-07 | Added a `scripts/generate-test-summary.ts`-generated summary page alongside the full `@vitest/ui` report - that report's own Pass/Fail/Slow dashboard lives in a compiled third-party client bundle, not something to reskin by hand-patching a dependency. First version rendered Pass/Fail as a two-segment donut (a meter against the total, matching the dataviz skill's guidance against a bare 2-slice pie) - technically correct but told the reader nothing beyond "100%" whenever nothing's failing, the common case. Redesigned around the dimension that actually varies: each module's share of the suite (store 798, effects 404, entity 141, operators 68), a genuine part-to-whole categorical composition, with pass/fail/slow moved to a stat row instead of forced into slices (status flags on tests already counted in some module's wedge, not disjoint categories - mixing them in would double-count). Colors are the project's validated categorical slots 1-4 in a fixed order tied to the order modules were added, not sorted by count, so a module's color stays stable across runs. Side finding while chasing a clean local preview: 4 consecutive full-suite runs hit the known `spec/types/**` flaky-timeout pattern at default worker parallelism, then passed clean on the first try at `--maxWorkers=2` - each of those tests spawns its own `tsc`/`vue-tsc` process, so more concurrent workers means more compiler processes competing for the same cores, not less contention. CI itself stayed reliably green throughout (confirmed via `gh run list` before assuming otherwise) - a local-machine-under-load observation, not a change to the repo's actual test config. |

| —     | `f78b930` | 2026-08-08 | `schematics:test`: reproduced deterministically by re-running the identical failed CI job twice with zero code changes (both failed the same way) — ruled out the usual local-only worker-contention flakiness pattern before looking for a real bug. Root cause: consolidating `ng-add/**` into `modules/schematics` dropped its `tsconfig.schematics.json` `include` down to `migrations/**/*.ts` only, so `ng-add`'s compiled output came solely from `build-package`'s `@nx/js:tsc` — which, unlike a bare `tsc` invocation, won't emit a file outside its own project root, so `schematics-core` silently never reached `dist/`. This had passed locally the entire session only because other modules' builds had already populated `dist/modules/schematics-core` as an incidental side effect; never true from CI's clean checkout. Fixed by restoring `ng-add/**/*.ts` to the include, verified against a fully nuked `dist/` + `node_modules/@ngrx/*` (not just `--skip-nx-cache`, which leaves already-built files alone) and a full `yarn test:report`: 293/293 files, 0 failures. **This fix turned out to be necessary but not sufficient** — see `ebb4288` below.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| —     | `ebb4288` | 2026-08-08 | Pushed `f78b930` expecting it to be the fix; CI failed again with the identical `Cannot find module '../../../schematics-core/utility/standalone'` error. Dug one level deeper: `schematics-core` has never had a build target of its own — `dist/modules/schematics-core` has only ever existed as an incidental side effect of whichever _consuming_ module's own `tsc` invocation happened to compile a file that imports it, and 8 different modules' `build` targets all separately declared that same path as one of their own outputs. Under Nx's remote caching, several tasks claiming the same output is genuinely ambiguous — a stale cache restore for any one of them can silently overwrite a freshly (and correctly) produced `schematics-core` with old content. Confirmed exactly that: CI's `schematics:build-package` showed a real cache miss (the `f78b930` fix ran), but `schematics:build` — the target that actually needed `schematics-core` on disk — came back a **remote cache hit from before the fix existed**. Fixed architecturally: gave `schematics-core` a real, independently-cacheable `build` target (reusing `tsconfig.lib.json` — the exact file phase 38's `bd4d5be` entry above had called "dormant, used by no build target," now put to its intended use), wired `schematics`'s `build-package` to depend on it explicitly, and removed the redundant output claim from all 8 consuming modules so exactly one task owns that path. Verified against the specific failure class, not just a fresh build: deleted `dist/`, built once to populate Nx's _local_ cache, deleted `dist/` again, rebuilt relying on cache hits (19/19 hit) — `schematics-core` came back intact. Full `yarn test:report`: 293/293 files, 3529/3530 tests (1 skipped), 0 failures. |
| —     | `df331cb` | 2026-08-08 | With CI's real caching bug fixed, the one remaining failure was `src/container/index.spec.ts` — 4 snapshot mismatches, but a much smaller, genuinely different problem from everything above it. Root-caused by instrumenting the actual generated content directly (not just reasoning about it): the constructor-insertion `ReplaceChange` hardcoded `oldText: '\n'` (a 1-character removal) at the empty class body's `members.pos`, assuming the underlying `@schematics/angular`-generated content always uses a bare `\n` there. On Windows it doesn't — the base template comes through as `\r\n` — so removing 1 byte only strips the `\r` half of the pair, leaving a stray `\n` that renders as an extra blank line between the inserted constructor and the class's closing brace; on Linux the same 1-byte removal correctly consumes the real `\n`, so CI's output was clean and the snapshot (recorded on Windows) didn't match it. Deliberately not fixed by picking whichever platform's output the snapshot happened to match — that would only flip which platform breaks. Fixed in the schematic itself: detect the actual line terminator present at that position and remove its real length instead of assuming 1. Produces identical, correct output on both platforms now — verified 20/20 container tests passing locally (previously failing with the blank line present) against the same content CI already treated as correct.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| —     | `b177eed` | 2026-08-08 | TS6 flagged `modules/schematics/tsconfig.build.json`: "The common source directory ... is '..' . The 'rootDir' setting must be explicitly set." | —     | —      | 2026-08-08 | Real, previously-undiscovered bug, found while widening `tsconfig.schematics.json`'s `rootDir` the same way already done for `data`/`schematics`: compiling `migrations/**/*.ts` also pulled in every file under `events/` with `TS2307: Cannot find module '@ngrx/signals'`, even though no migration imports `events/` at all — confirmed by tracing each migration's import graph individually (none reached it) and with `tsc --traceResolution`. The actual cause: `@types/node/process.d.ts` does `import { EventEmitter } from 'events'` (Node's real built-in module), and this tsconfig's `baseUrl: "."` — a leftover from a `paths` alias removed as part of the same fix — made TypeScript check `modules/signals/` for that bare specifier before falling through to Node's types, and `modules/signals/events/` (this package's own secondary entry point, a genuine naming collision) won the resolution. `exclude`-ing `events/**` didn't help, confirming it wasn't a glob-inclusion problem but real import reachability, which `exclude` can't block. Fixed by removing the now-unneeded `baseUrl` entirely.                                                                                                                                  |
| —     | —      | 2026-08-08 | Composition-over-inheritance audit: the only class inheritance in the module is `Events`/`ReducerEvents extends BaseEvents`, and `BaseEvents` itself is a plain abstract class that privately composes a `Subject`/`Observable` rather than extending one — already matches this repo's own composition-over-inheritance design, consistent with how `component-store`'s one real class needed no changes either. Error-handling audit: zero `catch` blocks that swallow anything — the two bare `catch {}` blocks are a standard Angular idiom (`inject(Injector)` throws outside an injection context; the catch intentionally falls back to `undefined`), and every `throw` is descriptive at a real validation boundary. Verified: `nx build signals` (clean), `nx build schematics` (clean, `signals-ng-add` resolves `schematics-core` correctly), `nx lint signals` (0 errors, 71 pre-existing warnings), `nx lint schematics` (0 errors, 16 warnings — unchanged baseline, `signals-ng-add` added none). Full `yarn test:report`: 4431/4440 tests (1 skipped, 8 `spec/types/**` timeouts under the now-larger 413-file combined suite, all confirmed clean in isolation — the same established local-only worker-contention pattern, not a regression). |

All 8 flagged packages were transitive (pulled in via `nx`, `@angular/cli`, `firebase-functions`, `conventional-changelog-cli` — none were direct `package.json` dependencies), so every fix went through a root `resolutions` block rather than a plain `yarn upgrade`. Grouped one PR per vulnerable package rather than one per alert or one giant PR, so each is bisectable to a single dependency: `qs` → 6.15.3 ([#25](https://github.com/Terrence721/platform-main/pull/25), closes 3 alerts at once), `braces` → 3.0.3 ([#26](https://github.com/Terrence721/platform-main/pull/26)), `@hono/node-server` → 2.x ([#28](https://github.com/Terrence721/platform-main/pull/28)), `esbuild` → 0.28.1+ ([#29](https://github.com/Terrence721/platform-main/pull/29)), `uuid` → 11.1.1 ([#30](https://github.com/Terrence721/platform-main/pull/30)). |

A blanket `resolutions` entry would have force-crossed those to a major version they were never built against. Used yarn's nested-path resolution syntax (`**/nx/brace-expansion`) to target only the vulnerable copy — verified after `yarn install` with `yarn why brace-expansion` that the scoped copy merged into the patched version while the unrelated copies stayed untouched.                                                                                                                                                                                               |

Same scoped-resolution technique (`**/compare-func/dot-prop`) rather than a blanket override, which would have force-downgraded `nitropack`'s copy to a version with a different API and risked breaking it.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |

Rather than leave them silently open or guess at a workaround, dismissed both with `dismissed_reason: tolerable_risk` and an explicit comment noting there's nothing to fix yet and to recheck once `image-size` ships a patched release.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |

Closed it without duplicating any logic back out — Angular CLI's real `ng add <package>` resolution (that package's own `package.json` → its own `"schematics"` collection → a schematic literally named `"ng-add"`) can point at a thin per-module wrapper delegating to the already-shared implementation. Gave each of the 11 modules (`component`, `component-store`, `data`, `effects`, `entity`, `eslint-plugin`, `operators`, `router-store`, `signals`, `store`, `store-devtools`) its own `collection.json` — a single `"ng-add"` entry, `factory`/`schema` pointing via relative path at `../schematics/ng-add/<name>`, reusing the exact description text already in the shared `modules/schematics/collection.json` — plus `"schematics": "./collection.json"` and `"ng-add": { "save": "dependencies" }` (`"devDependencies"` for `eslint-plugin` only, matching `modules/schematics`'s own self-referential convention for dev tooling) added to each `package.json`, and `"@ngrx/schematics": "0.0.0"` added to each `peerDependencies` block — the exact convention this repo already uses for every other cross-module `@ngrx/*` reference (confirmed against `store-devtools`/`data`'s existing `@ngrx/store` peer entries before assuming it was the right shape). |

Real, pre-existing gap found: every other module's `build` target populates `node_modules/@ngrx/<name>`; `modules/schematics`'s never did, so `@ngrx/schematics` had never once been materialized as a real sibling package. Added the missing `rimraf`/`mkdirp`/`ncp` trio, matching every other module's exact pattern ([#283](https://github.com/Terrence721/platform-main/pull/283), merged first since every other module's PR depends on it). ### CI reliability: two Nx-remote-cache races surfaced by the ng-add rollout

| Phase | Commit | Date       | What                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| ----- | ------ | ---------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| 45    | —      | 2026-09-02 | Landing the 14 PRs above kept intermittently hitting `TS2307: Cannot find module '@ngrx/store'` in `effects`/`entity`/`data`'s `build-package` steps — a build task getting a Cache Miss immediately after `store`'s own build showed a Remote Cache Hit in the same Nx Cloud task table, right as another workflow's build was doing the same work concurrently. The same underlying class of Nx-remote-cache-restore-timing issue phase 32 already diagnosed and partly fixed once before, for `schematics-core` specifically. Ruled out a logic bug first, not last: the dependency graph (`nx graph`) resolves `effects → store` as a real, correct `static` edge every time locally, and every single failure cleared cleanly on retry with zero code changes — never once a genuine, reproducible-in-isolation break.                                                                                                                                       |
| —     | —      | 2026-09-02 | Root cause 1, found and fixed ([#289](https://github.com/Terrence721/platform-main/pull/289)): `quality.yml` triggered on both `push` and `pull_request` with no branch filter, so every commit on a PR branch spun up two full, concurrent Quality pipelines for the identical commit SHA, both independently hitting the same shared Nx Cloud remote cache at nearly the same time. Scoped `push` to `branches: [main]`, matching the pattern `pages.yml` already used — a PR branch now gets exactly one Quality pipeline instead of two racing each other.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| —     | —      | 2026-09-02 | Root cause 2, found and fixed ([#290](https://github.com/Terrence721/platform-main/pull/290)): even after #289, the very next push to `main` failed the identical way twice more — `pages.yml`'s own "Build report" job (a full `nx run-many --target=build --all`) was still running concurrently with `quality.yml`'s push-triggered run on every push to `main`, since `pages.yml` had its own independent `push: branches: [main]` trigger. Switched `pages.yml` from `push` to `workflow_run` on Quality's completion, so Deploy Pages only starts once Quality has fully finished — sequential, never concurrent. Gated on `workflow_run.conclusion == 'success'` (a real behavior change: a failed Quality run no longer deploys a report built from code that just failed its own tests) and checks out `workflow_run.head_sha` explicitly so it always builds the exact commit Quality validated, not just whatever `main`'s HEAD happens to be by then. |
| —     | —      | 2026-09-02 | Confirmed both fixes actually work, not just "looks right": #291 through #296 (6 module PRs landed after #289) each passed clean on the first CI attempt, zero retries — a sharp contrast to #287/#288 needing 2-3 retries each before #289 existed. After #290 merged, two back-to-back `main` merges each produced sequential `Quality → Deploy Pages` runs (confirmed via `gh run list`, one `workflow_run`-triggered Deploy Pages queued behind the other under `pages.yml`'s own `concurrency: group: pages` setting) — both fully green, no races, no retries needed.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |

Re-verifying that note while starting `find-component.ts`'s own review surfaced a real, previously-undiscovered bug the first pass missed: every one of `findModuleFromOptions`'s 9 real call sites in `modules/schematics` guarded the call itself with `if (options.module) { ... }`, so its own internal auto-detect branch (`findModule()`, walk up to the nearest ancestor module) could never run — confirmed via a real, pre-existing snapshot showing `app-module.ts` completely untouched after a schematic run with no `--module` flag, contradicting both the function's own doc comment and the test's own name. Filed [#307](https://github.com/Terrence721/platform-main/issues/307), fixed for `store`/`reducer`/`entity`/`feature` (only passive snapshot coverage, no test asserting no-wiring as intended) and the 5 one-time `ng-add/*` integration schematics — all now call `findModuleFromOptions` unconditionally. Deliberately left `effect`/`component-store` unfixed: both have an explicit, deliberately-named test (`should not be provided by default`) asserting omitted-`--module` should produce no wiring as a real, considered contract, not incidental coverage — confirmed with the repo owner directly rather than assumed, given repeated feature-level generation carries materially higher risk of the "nearest ancestor module" heuristic guessing wrong in a real multi-module app, and `component-store` has `--component` as its other, more idiomatic target anyway. `feature`'s own schema gained a real `skipImport` option, forwarded to its nested `entity`/`reducer` calls, since 5 tests targeting the `'baz'` library fixture (a real Angular library, no root `NgModule` at all) needed an explicit way to opt out — the throw they'd otherwise now correctly hit is intended behavior for a library target, not a bug to route around. Corrected the [PR #306](https://github.com/Terrence721/platform-main/pull/306)/`find-module.ts` `docs/code-review.md` entry, which had wrongly claimed the auto-detect path was already transitively covered. Verified: full repo build/lint clean across all 13 modules; `npx vitest run modules/schematics modules/schematics-core` (56 files / 444 tests, all passing, including 7 manually-reviewed `store` snapshot updates showing real `StoreModule.forRoot()`/`.forFeature()` wiring, not blindly accepted); a full clean-cache repo-wide run showed 25 failing files, 23 the documented pre-existing `spec/types/**` flakiness class and 2 `eslint-plugin` `RuleTester` tests that reproduced clean in isolation — zero failures in anything this fix actually touched. |

### Comparing against upstream: what's still not here, and why

Everything above this point was found by working forward from this repo's own history. This section works the other direction — a direct comparison against the real upstream ngrx/platform source tree, checking for anything that exists there but was never carried over here, whether or not it was ever explicitly decided. Two different outcomes came out of that comparison, and they're tracked differently on purpose: real gaps worth eventually closing (issues, Backlog column), versus scope that was never going to be ported and isn't going to be, documented so a reader sees the decision rather than an unexplained absence.

**Real gap, now closed — `@ngrx/router-store/data-persistence` ([#301](https://github.com/Terrence721/platform-main/issues/301)):** upstream's `router-store` module ships a second entry point, `data-persistence/`, exporting four effects operators — `pessimisticUpdate`, `optimisticUpdate`, `fetch`, `navigation` (all `@deprecated` upstream itself, but still shipped and still used by real consumers). Every other module's `src/` directory was confirmed complete against upstream; this was the one exception — silently missed when `router-store` was added in phase 34, not a decision anyone made. It's the most substantive actual feature gap this comparison turned up. Ported 2026-10-01 (#301), as a supported API: not deprecated here.

**Not started for a long time — `projects/example-app`/`example-app-e2e` ([#302](https://github.com/Terrence721/platform-main/issues/302)) and `projects/standalone-app`/`standalone-app-e2e` ([#303](https://github.com/Terrence721/platform-main/issues/303)):** upstream's two consumer demo apps — one module-based (`auth`/`books`/`material`/`reducers` feature modules), one standalone-components-based — each with its own Playwright e2e suite. This repo's scope has always been the 13 library packages under `modules/`, never the `projects/` consumer apps that demonstrate them; `nx.json`'s dead `"defaultProject": "example-app"` reference (only removed in [PR #300](https://github.com/Terrence721/platform-main/pull/300)) is a leftover from the original bootstrap copy, not evidence anything here was ever built out and abandoned. Both stayed Backlog because an app proves the modules work together end-to-end, not just in isolation. On 2026-10-01 [#303](https://github.com/Terrence721/platform-main/issues/303) started as a port of `standalone-app`, and on 2026-10-02 it was redirected into the Helpdesk app (`projects/helpdesk`): a real help desk with a NestJS API, PostgreSQL and sign-in that uses every module, rather than a copy of the demo. `example-app` ([#302](https://github.com/Terrence721/platform-main/issues/302)) is still Backlog.

**Permanently out of scope, not just undone — `projects/www` and the npm-publish `build/` tooling ([#304](https://github.com/Terrence721/platform-main/issues/304), closed as a documentation record):** upstream's `projects/www` is the actual ngrx.io documentation website; `build/deploy-build.ts`, `publish-next.ts`, `publish-latest.ts`, `publish-release.ts`, `stackblitz.ts`, and friends are the tooling that publishes all 13 packages to npm and regenerates that site's content. Neither has a place here on purpose, not by oversight: this repo already has its own documentation surface for a portfolio-review audience (this file, `docs/`, the wiki, `portfolio.html`) — a second, differently-scoped docs site would just be two systems telling two stories about the same code — and `package.json` has been `"private": true`, version `0.0.0`, since the de-affiliation pass (phase 10), so there has never been anything to publish. [PR #300](https://github.com/Terrence721/platform-main/pull/300)'s removal of the `package.json` scripts that pointed at this exact `build/` directory (`deploy:builds`, `copy:schematics`, `build:stackblitz`, `publish:next`, `publish:latest`, `update:versions`) was the visible symptom; this is the fuller reason the directory itself was never carried over in the first place.

### Code review — per-file tracking

Process detail: [docs/code-review.md](docs/code-review.md). One module was worked at a time; each module's table stays here as the record of its review, followed by the `migrations/` review ([#106](https://github.com/Terrence721/platform-main/issues/106)). All of them are complete.

`effects` completed 2026-08-19 (18/18 files, 0 real bugs found — [#36](https://github.com/Terrence721/platform-main/issues/36), closed). `router-store` completed 2026-08-25 (12/12 files, 7 real bugs found and fixed, the highest hit rate of any module so far — [#38](https://github.com/Terrence721/platform-main/issues/38), closed). `store-devtools` completed 2026-08-27 (11/11 files, 6 real bugs + 1 minor cleanup found and fixed — [#39](https://github.com/Terrence721/platform-main/issues/39), closed). `component-store` completed 2026-08-29 (4/4 files, 1 real gap found and fixed — [#41](https://github.com/Terrence721/platform-main/issues/41), closed once merged). `component` completed 2026-08-31 (10/10 files, 1 real bug + 2 barrel-export gaps found and fixed — [#44](https://github.com/Terrence721/platform-main/issues/44), closed once merged). `operators` completed 2026-08-31 (4/4 files, 0 real bugs + 2 barrel-export gaps found and fixed — [#37](https://github.com/Terrence721/platform-main/issues/37), closed once merged). `schematics-core` completed 2026-09-17 (16/16 files, 9 real bugs + 2 test-coverage gaps + 13 barrel-export gaps (the largest barrel-gap finding of the whole audit) found and fixed — [#34](https://github.com/Terrence721/platform-main/issues/34), closed). `signals` completed 2026-09-26 (18/18 files, 14 defects found and fixed in 11 files, 1 barrel-export gap and 2 test-coverage gaps closed — [#43](https://github.com/Terrence721/platform-main/issues/43), closed).

#### `schematics-core` (module tracking issue [#34](https://github.com/Terrence721/platform-main/issues/34)) — started 2026-09-02, completed 2026-09-17

Chosen next after `operators` closed: smallest remaining module (16 files) among the 5 left under #32, matching the "pick the smallest remaining module" pattern used for every prior pick. Notable wrinkle going in: this module started with zero dedicated test coverage of its own (no vitest config, no `test` target) — it is a pure shared utility library exercised only indirectly through every other module's own migrations/ng-add tests, so each file's review needs to verify transitive coverage rather than trust a same-named spec file the way other modules could. `package.ts`'s review (#279) gave the module its own `vitest.config.mts`/`tsconfig.spec.json`/`test-setup.ts`/`test` target so a genuinely untested security guard could get a direct unit test — later files in this module can now add direct specs where warranted, though transitive coverage remains the default expectation.

The last module under #32.
